## [2026-11-12] Todo 133 progress (W=/home/andrico/kcc-wt/t133)
- Orientation: CodeGraph is unavailable because the repository has no index, so source inspection uses direct reads/searches. The audit append lock remains exclusive for the entire reload/verify/append/sync transaction; the planned retry change only changes when a contender gives up, never the mutual-exclusion boundary.
- Fairness argument: bounded exponential backoff reduces repeated lock-file polling under load, a small PID/attempt phase offset keeps independently started processes from waking in permanent synchrony, and the longer bounded window lets the current owner finish a complete durable append. There is no strict OS-level FIFO guarantee, so the bound remains finite and returns `AppendLockTimeout`; critically, that timeout affects only its append and does not invalidate writer state.
- Entry-point inventory under review: workflow `ChangeWorkflow::{begin,begin_migration}`; CLI `Ctx::{target,target_named}` and `change::run_completed`; MCP `support::access::begin_change` / `support::raw_change::{run,run_completed}`; Web `Operation::open`, `Action::{context,run,run_with}`, and `page_kit::change::apply_with`; domain interface guard; migration/preset `Applier::start`. Each path that can send a router request before its first audit append must observe audit liveness first.
- Final entry prechecks: `ChangeWorkflow::{begin,begin_migration}` owns the universal write preflight; CLI additionally checks in `Ctx::target_named` before client/detection requests; MCP checks before catalog/context/change construction; Web `Operation::open` refuses 503 before target facts and `change::apply_with` rechecks immediately before opening the change. Migration/preset `Applier::start` reaches `begin_migration`; the interface guard runs inside an already-prechecked `ChangeSet`.
- Recovery closure: CLI `change::run_completed`, MCP `support::raw_change::run_completed`, Web `page_kit::change::apply_with`, and `interfaces::guard::apply` now call the shared `recovery::finish` rule after any arm/work failure and only report `fail_safe_armed` when cleanup itself cannot be confirmed. Existing per-surface bracket suites remain green after updating stale tests that expected an intentionally armed timer on a reachable refusal.
- Permanent failure choice: `kcc serve` races the listener against `AuditLog::closed`; a fatal writer failure returns non-zero so the shipped Quadlet `Restart=on-failure` policy restarts it. `/readyz` still exposes `audit_alive` during the transition. Monitoring and Security docs describe this behavior.
- Red-first and mutation receipts: `lock_timeout_fails_one_append_and_writer_accepts_the_next` failed on the old fatal classification; fixed/restored `audit.rs` SHA-256 `abbea78bc15ac1640b4cbdd287b9de21d6c30d6825fb837ca7802dc05d50f90e`, fatal-timeout mutant `7a72adcd64be86759c8fef9d57be2e95b81c25d2a639ab4e439d6f966918da81`. `dead_audit_writer_refuses_begin_without_a_router_request` failed when the preflight was neutralized; fixed/restored `workflow.rs` `4a076eb283b4bc456e00d900457270d92eb5d06d0a926f53a01bb5f68f7bc72f`, mutant `c19a6ab402ad28d867b5949cd62422b682b9cc070661cb60e4cacd7d6b220f4d`. Interface cleanup mutant skipped disarm and failed the MCP real-surface assertion; fixed/restored `guard.rs` `eccbd9f4e1e668fb2828ba97ce1499ba1584a56ac40ff07aa09c948268aac796`, mutant `dbee7c6861bcc49a8a30c7ff72fc0193e763271e582db3beea229bb59b63aaa4`.
- Verification: `cargo test -p kcc-app -p kcc-mcp -p kcc-web` and `cargo test -p kcc --test cli` pass (CLI 78/78); dated-nightly fmt, five-crate all-target/all-feature Clippy with `-D warnings`, allow-gate, and cancel-safety pass. LSP timed out twice as expected; compiler/Clippy diagnostics are clean. No i18n key changed.

## [2026-11-12] Todo 129 progress (W=/home/andrico/kcc-wt/t129)
- Job ids are now `job-<12 lowercase hex Unix seconds>-<8 lowercase hex pid>-<8 lowercase hex OS entropy>-<12 lowercase hex process sequence>` (55 bytes). The fixed-width time prefix keeps lexical time order; pid, per-process entropy, and sequence prevent same-second cross-process and in-process repeats; lowercase hex and hyphens remain filename-safe.
- Consumer/validator audit: `crates/kcc-app/src/jobs.rs:validate_id` accepts 1..=64 lowercase ASCII letters/digits/hyphens; `crates/kcc-mcp/src/support/validate.rs:valid_job_id` has the same 64-byte/character contract; `crates/kcc-web/src/migrate_wizard/ids.rs:is_id` validates separate migration/preset ids (12..=40 lowercase hex/digits/hyphens), not JobRunner ids; CLI passes returned job ids without an independent validator; Web SSE paths and tests only require the `job-` prefix; persisted filenames and `job-owner-<id>` state lock names both remain under their 64-byte component bound.
- Collision truth: `JobOwnership::claim` maps an occupied id lock to `JobError::IdCollision`; `Applier::start` audits `migration.start` with outcome `refused` and reason `job_id_collision` before any worker/router request, then returns `ApplyError::JobStart`. CLI localizes `migrate-job-start-failed`; MCP returns `job_unavailable`; Web migration and preset views use the same retry message.
- Red-first and mutation proof: `same_second_allocations_from_different_processes_are_distinct` first failed with both ids `job-1800000000-1`; removing the process component later failed again. Fixed/restored `job_id.rs` SHA-256 `1ecd0d25eaf438d63a655238e82986534107898609082caf7af50e1b31605b12`, mutant `529f2d6ca74668d847fed3a4d2e702e2d1cdda4b2da634dc2860b45d94c7bb13`. `forced_identifier_collision_is_retryable_before_work_starts` failed when an occupied lock was remapped to generic state; fixed/restored `job_ownership.rs` SHA-256 `029599b4e8605ef51419468236a21222d855438d5526e7f64b66011999017bff`, mutant `b83f7dfdbaaee9bcac795d39dca7901c1fc45150478523c78258896751b31129`.
- Verification: `cargo test -p kcc-app -p kcc-mcp -p kcc-web` passed all non-live suites; `cargo test -p kcc --test cli` passed 76/76; dated-nightly fmt, five-crate all-target/all-feature Clippy with `-D warnings`, allow-gate, and i18n-check passed. LSP was explicitly excluded by the task; compiler and strict Clippy diagnostics are clean. The programming-skill checker reported false positives on inherited multiline `#[expect(... reason = ...)]` attributes; strict Clippy and allow-gate accepted them.

## [2026-11-11] Todo 116 progress (W=/home/andrico/kcc-wt/t116)
- `BatchChangeResult` now keeps `applied_management_changes` beside unchanged `applied_changes`; only successful validated items whose `contains_management()` is true feed the recovery applied fact. CLI, MCP, and Web therefore report outcome-unknown-armed when the management item was refused even if an ordinary sibling succeeded, while preserving per-item results, exit 7, audit `fail_safe_armed`, and fake-router armed state in router and extender modes.

Explicit recovery failures in `change.rs` already become `Failure` and now receive the same JSON field centrally.
- Red-first surface tests: CLI, MCP, and Web `refused_management_item_reports_unknown_armed` all failed on the old applied wording/code, then passed after the shared counter and call-site changes. Existing row-112 batch matrices were corrected so refused-management/applied-ordinary expects unknown rather than applied. CLI coverage includes all-refused and partially-refused batches in text and JSON for both modes.
- Mutation receipts: `workflow_batch.rs` fixed/restored SHA-256 `e2baf07e8d8fb5c00abe3e98ef6ae4903eefba00bf20f21f4c4dc7ee3091eb28`; forcing every successful change to count as management produced `139447f2dee36c0ab95f30bd4e04e585bff937d4741746f17715cd15db37a83e` and failed the MCP regression with `management_applied_fail_safe_armed`. `output.rs` fixed/restored SHA-256 `6003ebd472c8c400dbfa5baf2511e2cb2dd18e79c53868e0cbb857af53436da6`; suppressing the armed field produced `935adc98b0232cade6df358bf4000f272de8a58e9bd44259adacef8135f96d75` and failed the CLI regression because `/error/fail_safe_armed` was absent.

## [2026-11-10] Todo 102 progress (W=/home/andrico/kcc-wt/t102)

- `config failsafe` now dispatches on `(action, safety_mode)`: arm remains `SafetyNet::arm`; router commit remains byte-for-byte `SafetyNet::commit`; scheduled-reboot commit mirrors preset finish by saving running configuration then disarming with `no system reboot`; rollback remains `SafetyNet::rollback` in both modes, matching preset finish and the existing explicit rollback behavior. The existing committed/rolled-back messages remain truthful, so no locale or generated-key changes were needed. The existing JSON `applied(!matches!(action, Commit))` remains correct: scheduled commit saves (`unsaved: false`), while rollback continues reporting the row-101 follow-up behavior unchanged.
- Caller verdicts: `kcc/src/cli/persist.rs` was mode-blind only for commit and is fixed; its rollback was already intentionally identical to preset finish. `kcc/src/cli/preset/finish.rs` is the reference-correct mode-aware save path and mode-independent explicit rollback. `kcc-web/config_page/safety.rs` commit and rollback are native-only because `while_armed` requires `show last-change.fail-safe.active`, which the scheduled-reboot fallback does not expose; no change. `kcc/src/cli/migrate.rs` is a kept native migration finish path and remains native commit/rollback. `kcc-migrate/apply/finish.rs` already handles commit and rollback by mode (save+disarm and `system reboot` for scheduled mode). `kcc-migrate/apply/preflight.rs`, `apply/components.rs`, `kcc-domain/interfaces/guard.rs`, `kcc-web/page_kit/change.rs`, `kcc-mcp/generic/raw_change.rs`, and `kcc/src/cli/change.rs` pair arm with mode-aware disarm and have no blind commit/rollback. The remaining `SafetyNet::arm` occurrence is verification coverage only.
- Red-first regression `failsafe::scheduled_reboot_commit_saves_and_disarms` observed commands `["system reboot 900", "system configuration fail-safe commit"]` before the fix. New green tests are `scheduled_reboot_commit_saves_and_disarms`, `scheduled_reboot_rollback_remains_explicit`, and `router_commit_remains_native`; they assert fake-router command logs/configuration state, and the commit test advances the fake clock to prove no reboot remains scheduled.
- Mutation restored mode-blind commit and made `scheduled_reboot_commit_saves_and_disarms` fail for the intended missing `no system reboot`. `persist.rs` SHA-256 fixed before mutation/restored: `0bd17546bfb3b2b03909017d62028b32f38b8aa3fd7217b37531cb365b5f02c9`; mutated: `ec99fb3bdfd0d9bb2698d3c97bd245d313aec642ab0798534634d105cd85ece7`.
- Verification: full CLI integration 41/41 green after formatting; full `kcc-domain` and doc tests green; strict all-target/all-feature Clippy for kcc/kcc-domain/kcc-testkit/kcc-i18n green; dated-nightly fmt, i18n-check (`0 problem(s)` across six locales), and cancel-safety (`0 of 191`) green. LSP diagnostics timed out on both changed files; compiler and strict Clippy diagnostics were clean. No kcc-testkit production change, i18n key, or `docs/MCP_TOOLS.md` change was required. No live hardware exists, so scheduled reboot behavior remains simulator/documentation-based.

| Background work | Started at | Role | Why |

| Unix SIGHUP reload loop | `crates/kcc/src/server.rs:166`, spawned in `with_reload` at `crates/kcc/src/server.rs:491-512` | Serve only | It owns long-running HTTP transport reload state; stdio does not call `with_reload`. |

## Todo 15: config text and NS3
- NS3 encode selects a total block length of 18, 36, or 72 bytes from `plaintext_len + 3`; appends NUL and deterministic MD5-derived filler; applies the Fibonacci-stream XOR then payload-wide XOR mask; inserts MD5 bytes 0 and 1 at `x2 % (length - 1)` and `length / 7`; Base64 encodes. Decode removes markers in reverse order, applies the same two involutions, and truncates at NUL. The PowerShell Gallery script says `length / 8`, but the public Python reference, the verified todo-53 shell port, and all three KATs require `length / 7`.
- Config parsing ignores non-header `!` lines, detects the smallest positive indentation as one level (covering 1- and 4-space exports), rejects non-multiples and skipped levels, and treats line numbers as source metadata rather than AST identity so canonical print/parse equality is structural.
- Tokenization unescapes only `\\"` and `\\\\` inside quoted arguments and folds `ns3 <blob>` into `Token::Ns3`; canonical printing re-quotes empty/whitespace/quote/backslash-containing words, always uses one-space levels, and writes `!` after every top-level block.
- Full workspace fmt can be transiently blocked by concurrent agents' unformatted `kcc-app`/`kcc-testkit` edits; crate-scoped fmt plus strict clippy/tests/rustdoc verifies todo 15 without touching those owned paths.

## Todo 7

- Treating `RciPath::from_str` as one segment is essential: multi-segment paths use `RciPath::from_segments`, so an object name such as `WifiMaster0/AccessPoint0` cannot silently become a path. Iterative status traversal stores `(JsonPointer, &Value)` on a `Vec` stack and skips descending into the consumed `status` member.
- The strict lint profile requires `#[inline]` on public trait methods, `# Errors` docs even for private fallible helpers, and narrow `#[expect]` annotations on assertion-based tests. A manual `Debug` implementation for `RciError` prevents retained router messages from leaking.

## Todo 56: startup privilege floor
- Linux mirrors krb-passwd-ui's strict procfs contract and clear order: Ambient, Bounding, Inheritable, Effective, Permitted. On ordinary hosts a non-root process cannot clear a populated bounding set without `CAP_SETPCAP`, so only a reported-success bounding drop requires `CapBnd == 0`; `no_new_privs` makes inherited file privilege inert and the source guard forbids subprocess execution.
- Podman 5.7.0 matrix with the musl binary: `--user 0` refused with exit 77 and all four UIDs reported as 0; `--user 1000 --cap-drop=ALL` exited 0; `--user 1000` with default capabilities exited 0 after the binary's fresh `/proc/self/status` verification. Reading `/proc/self/status` from a shell after `/kcc` exits reports the shell's `NoNewPrivs: 0`, not the completed child; the successful binary return itself proves its internal post-read observed 1.
- Passwordless sudo was already confirmed unavailable, so the sudo scenario was logged as skipped and no sudo command was attempted.

## Todo 13: catalog parser and committed data
- Bookmark headings are authoritative, but Appendix C is not always NVOX: older manuals also bookmark cryptographic profile tables as `C.*`. Accept Appendix C commands only when the path is `nvox` or starts with `nvox `; require the exact heading outside dot-leader TOC rows and `Description` within the next three non-empty lines.
- Poppler layout usually separates Arguments columns by two or more spaces, but rows such as `to-interface Interface` can have one space. Recover rows by matching the longest expected synopsis argument prefix, then recognize the closed value-type vocabulary before falling back to a keyword literal.
- Chapter 3 level-2 bookmarks plus `C.1 nvox` are the command-group index; top-level JSON shards are tracked separately. KeeneticOS 5.1 has 219 indexed command groups and 58 top-level shards in the extracted KN-1811/manual union.
- Union statistics: 4.3 = 1058 commands, 57 shards, KN-1811 synopsis 853/854 (99%), 7 warning-bearing commands; 5.0 = 1089 commands, 59 shards, 880/881 (99%), 8 warnings; 5.1 = 1114 commands, 58 shards, 906/907 (99%), 8 warnings.
- Generated Rust must be emitted in rustfmt's multiline tuple/include layout; running rustfmt after generation is insufficient because a later catalog build would recreate an unformatted committed artifact.

## [2026-11-05] Task: todo-16 (fake router v2)
- Running and startup configuration are separate `kcc_config::config_text::Ast` values inside each `Shared`; generated `/ci/*.txt` headers hash only the canonical command body, and JSON settings remain an independent store.
- Fail-safe expiry is checked at authorization/read boundaries against the injected `Clock`; expiry restores startup before clearing every session, making the triggering old cookie receive 401 without sleeps.
- Upload replaces startup only; `system reboot`, rollback, component commit, and timer expiry load startup into running and invalidate all sessions. The acceptance tests cover parse/save divergence, exact unknown-command no-op, upload/reboot, keep-alive/commit, and expiry rollback.
- Todo 53 had no live hardware, so `BEHAVIOUR.md` marks fail-safe, NS3 acceptance, and POST-only show behavior assumed and cites both `spikes/live-router/RESULT.md` and the task-53 JSON evidence.

## Todo 8: ndw2 session implementation
- The fake router emits space-separated ndw2 parameters while combined multi-scheme headers use commas between schemes and may use commas between parameters. The parser therefore first splits commas outside quotes, then classifies each chunk as a new scheme or a continuation and quote-safely splits whitespace-separated assignments.
- A synchronous mutex protects only the in-memory session snapshot; a Tokio mutex serializes authentication exchanges. Requests clone the session and release all locks before awaiting wire I/O. After a 401, the login lock is acquired and the failed cookie is compared with the current cookie so concurrent requests reuse the one replacement login.

- Verification blocker on 2026-11-05: concurrent uncommitted `kcc-testkit` additions (`src/config_state.rs`, `src/failsafe.rs`) failed compilation before `cargo test -p kcc-rci --test session` reached the task tests. Errors included construction/matching of non-exhaustive `kcc_config` types, unsupported digest `LowerHex`, and `Option<Duration>::map(Duration::as_secs)`. Todo 8 is scoped away from `kcc-testkit`, so these files were not modified.

## Todo 11: router capability detection
- `RouterInfo::detect(&SessionManager, &RouterUrl)`: `SessionManager` keeps its router URL and reqwest client private, and an authenticated `GET /auth` answers 200 with no `WWW-Authenticate`. The scheme probe therefore uses a separate unauthenticated reqwest client (no redirects, 10 s timeout) against the passed origin, and stores only scheme kinds, never challenge or session ids. When todo 10's `RciClient` lands, detect can take it instead.
- The fake router has no `show/system/mode` and no `show/interface/bands` fixture, so both answer 404, and detection records `None` (this is the bands-404 acceptance scenario). Its `show interface` has no `WifiMaster` entry, so radios come from the `WifiMasterN/...` id prefix.
- `FirmwareVersion` orders by (major, minor, stage, build, patch). Release letters map A=Alpha, B=Beta, C=Release (`5.01.C.3.0-0` == `5.1.3`); the title is parsed first, then the release. `module_name_repetitions` fires on `version::FirmwareVersion`, so it carries an `#[expect]`.
- Shared lib.rs with concurrent siblings: verify in a detached `git worktree` (shared `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`) holding only your files, then stage lib.rs via `git hash-object -w` + `git update-index --cacheinfo` so siblings' unstaged mod lines stay out of the commit.

## Todo 9: ndw4, experimental token, and digest authentication
- ndw4 follows R-RCI 1486b62: phase replies are base64 JSON headers, the first two may be HTTP 401, raw decimal strings are signed without normalization, and successful phase three is followed by GET /auth. Both error/e fields are checked independently. Signature mismatch and cost refusal never downgrade to ndw2.
- The key cache is bounded to one parameter set per immutable SessionManager (router/login/password identity), with Zeroizing client/stored/server keys. The process-wide Argon2 semaphore defaults to one; configure_argon2_concurrency sets it once before use. Its permit moves into spawn_blocking so cancellation cannot release the memory budget while hashing continues.
- The blocked-permit unit test proves cached re-login and local cost refusal never enter the Argon2 queue. The TypeScript/hash-wasm 4.12.0 vector reproduces all intermediates. Generator lives at crates/kcc-rci/scripts/vectors/ndw4-vector.mjs to respect the assigned crate scope.
- kcc-testkit::auth_fixture runs a separate loopback auth router with independent server-side verification, dummy users, iter=2/memcost=64, and counters containing no secret values. This avoids shared server.rs edits. Token support remains experimental; DigestProxy signs the actual method and URI including query, with MD5/SHA-256 and qop=auth.
- Dependency tree has no normal duplicates: OS randomness reuses getrandom 0.2.17 already required by ring; Argon2 0.6.0, HMAC 0.13.0, SHA3 0.11.0, subtle 2.6.1 retain one RustCrypto generation. Initial getrandom 0.4 addition was removed after tree inspection.
- Verification: 11 requested integration tests, vector/cache/cost unit tests, existing ndw2 tests, all kcc-rci/kcc-testkit tests, workspace fmt/clippy/doc/build, xtask clippy, allow-gate, and profile-eq pass. Deliberately inverted cost/signature guards caused regression failures and were restored byte-for-byte; restored ndw4 suite passed. Workspace tests stop at unrelated kcc/tests/source_guards.rs:32,49 because embedded CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR source paths cannot be read (NotFound). Every attempted LSP diagnostic timed out; compiler/Clippy diagnostics are clean. Logs: .omo/evidence/task-9-keenetic-control-center*.log.

Byte cap per item = `RciClientOptions::with_response_bytes(1 MiB)` plus a manual chunk cap on the root read.
- Anonymizer IPv4 design (prefix-preserving within a 768-address doc space): private/CGNAT `/24`s map first-seen to 192.0.2.0/24 then 198.51.100.0/24 with the host octet kept; every other address (public, or a third+ private `/24`) is pooled host-by-host into 203.0.113.1..254; netmask-shaped, loopback, multicast, link-local kept. IPv6: `/48` -> `2001:db8:N::/48` keeping the subnet id, IIDs > 0xffff HMAC'd. MAC: `02:00:00` + HMAC-SHA256(64-byte per-run key) low bytes with collision retry.
- Free text needs TWO passes over all captured items with one Anonymizer: the first only learns hostnames/SSIDs/serials/secrets from keyed JSON, the second also substring-replaces those learned originals in logs and unkeyed strings (serde_json Map iterates keys sorted, so a `log` key is visited before `serial`/`ssid`).
- Config text is anonymized structurally: `kcc_config::config_text::parse` -> `secrets::redact` -> every `Token::Ns3` payload -> `<redacted>`, alias words after `ssid`/`description`/`hostname`/`known host`/`ip host`/`domain name`; parse failure skips the item (fail closed) rather than writing a half-anonymized file.
- Scanner (`tests/no_secrets.rs`) walks the repo skipping `.git`, `target`, `.omo`, `.cache`, `~`, `node_modules`, `.codegraph` (the plan file under `.omo` contains `192.168.77.5`). ns3 rule = Base64 run after `ns3 `/`ns3 "` with length >= 24 and a multiple of 4 (ns3 blobs are 24/48/96 chars), so prose like `ns3 codec` is not flagged. Unit tests that need private IPs, foreign MACs or 44-char keys build them at run time with `format!`, otherwise the repo scan flags the test source itself; doc examples must use `02:00:00:..` MACs.

## [2026-11-06] Todo 28: migration classification rules
- `kcc-migrate::rules` embeds ordered TOML policy with a small segment glob (`*`, prefix `foo*`, `**`); evaluation is explicit rule, target catalog, explicit rule component requirement, then default copy. Catalog command detection selects the longest visible command path whose command words occur in order among flattened CLI words.
- Classification consumes `kcc_config::config_text::flatten`; source-order index plus line number makes the one-item-per-leaf invariant observable. A 256-case property test compares item indices and line numbers directly with parser-produced flattened lines.
- Reports use `kcc_config::secrets::redact` plus an unconditional typed-NS3 redaction pass; executable output is held separately in `SecretString`. Thus NS3 and WireGuard keys are copied unchanged for migration but never appear in `Debug`, `before`, or `after`.
- Realm hashes follow `ndw2 hash = md5(login:realm:password)` and are treated as realm-bound pending todo 31 live rehearsal. A differing realm requires an operator password and emits `user <name> password plain`; an equal realm copies the hash.

## [2026-11-06] Task: todo-35 (fleet, router forms, live dashboard)
- askama 0.16 argument borrowing: a bare variable or field passed to a function becomes `&(var)`, method-call results are passed by value. So `{{ t(field) }}` needs `t(*field)` and an `if let Some(x) = view.key()` binding needs `t(**x)`; comparisons need `*x == keys::...`. Templates derived in a module can read that module's private struct fields (FormPage reads Draft/FieldErrors fields directly), which avoids accessor boilerplate where `unreachable_pub` forbids `pub` fields in `pub(crate)` types.
- `#[tokio::test(start_paused = true)]` works against a real loopback FakeRouter: loopback readiness is immediate, so auto-advance never fires mid-request, and `spawn_blocking` (DNS, Argon2) inhibits auto-advance. The 31 s / 2-minute SSE requirements are asserted with `timeout_at` and `sleep(120 s)` + `sleep(600 s)` on `Handle::request_count()`; breaking `Feed::drop` (no abort) makes it fail (164 -> 874 requests).
- htmx-ext-sse 2.2.4 fires `htmx:sseBeforeMessage` on the `sse-swap` element; `preventDefault()` skips the swap, so one connection can carry HTML fragments and JSON. `hx-swap-oob` elements inside SSE fragments are processed. htmx settle copies `class`/`style` from old to new elements with the same id via setAttribute, which violates `style-src 'self'` when an extension or Playwright left `style=""`: app.js sets `htmx.config.attributesToSettle = ["class","width","height"]` (the `htmx-config` meta stays byte-identical for the foundation test). htmx does not swap 4xx by default; app.js swaps 422 form responses.
- uPlot 1.6.32 is CSP-clean (CSSOM only, no eval). Canvas strokes need resolved colours: read `getComputedStyle(el).color` of a swatch; `getPropertyValue("--token")` returns the raw `light-dark(...)` text, and an invalid strokeStyle silently keeps the previous colour (grid lines came out in the series colour).
- Playwright gotchas: `button[type=submit]` matches the masthead fleet-switcher "Open" button first; `file://` images do not load in `setContent` pages (embed data URLs); a `pgrep -f`/`pkill -f` pattern that appears in the tool's own command line kills the tool shell (find PIDs with `ps -eo pid,args | awk '/[v]isual_harness-/'`).
- rustdoc: an intra-doc link `[`format`]` to a module named `format` is ambiguous with `format!`; write `[`format`](mod@format)`.
- Visual QA without the frontend skill scripts (check-draft-guard, detect SDK not on disk): self-review of captures at 1280 light/dark and 390 phone (ru dark), CSP violation logging through `securitypolicyviolation` in an init script, and scrollWidth checks for sideways overflow.

## [2026-11-06] Todo 48 first atomic unit
- Tokio `Notify::notified()` must be pinned and `enable()`d before checking the protected active-job map; otherwise a completion between the check and first poll can be missed. The bounded drain uses `timeout_at` only as a circuit breaker, while task cleanup notifications are the synchronization event.
- `mpsc::Sender::is_closed()` is a cheap readiness accessor, but a writer loop must call `Receiver::close()` before replying with its terminal I/O error so the caller observes liveness false immediately and deterministically.
- Registry reload needs a stored effective config+state snapshot rather than recomputing the "previous" set from freshly read state; otherwise external state-file changes are invisible to the diff and their cached clients are not invalidated.
- Verification: nightly workspace format check, the three focused regression tests, and `cargo clippy -p kcc-app --lib --all-features -- -D warnings` pass. LSP diagnostics timed out. The standalone no-excuse script reports false positives on existing multiline `#[expect(... reason = ...)]` attributes; compiler and Clippy accept the reasons.

## [2026-11-06] Todo 48 process acceptance suite
- rmcp 3.x HTTP initialization negotiates `2025-06-18`; the request needs `Accept: application/json, text/event-stream`, and the initialized notification omits `params`. Streamable responses may be SSE: select the first nonempty `data:` payload and trim its leading space before JSON decoding.
- A detached stderr reader can race process exit and lose the final refusal line. Keep its JoinHandle, wait for the child, join the reader, then drain the channel before asserting stderr. The Drop path kills/reaps first and then joins.
- The reqwest test client uses `rustls-no-provider`; tests must install the existing ring provider before building a client. Adding direct test-only `rustls` reused 0.23.46 and introduced no dependency generation.
- Final focused evidence: `.omo/evidence/task-48-keenetic-control-center.log` contains 4/4 passing process tests. Dependency output was appended to `.omo/evidence/task-2-keenetic-control-center.log`.

Workspace tests reach an unrelated concurrent fake-router change: `kcc-rci/tests/capabilities.rs:140` expects `["WifiMaster0"]` while current testkit returns `["WifiMaster0", "WifiMaster1"]`. LSP diagnostics timed out; compiler/Clippy diagnostics for the changed kcc surfaces are clean.

## [2026-11-06] Todo 48 final review corrections
- Long-lived singleton ownership must use `try_lock`, not blocking `lock`, at process startup; blocking advisory locks turn duplicate-service configuration errors into silent hangs. Stdio is a per-client transport and must not participate in the singleton lock.
- Stdio acceptance is line-delimited JSON-RPC: retain piped stdin/stdout, write and flush each JSON line, read through a dedicated thread/channel with a deadline, close stdin to end the session, then require exit 0. Running it while HTTP serve holds the state verifies both lock semantics and the in-handler viewer fallback identity.
- Child-process hermeticity requires explicit environment injection even when the config has an explicit state directory: master-key resolution is independently HOME/XDG based. The test fixture now passes all four paths explicitly to every child, including refusal tests.
- Final verification appended to `.omo/evidence/task-48-keenetic-control-center.log`: 7/7 process tests and the complete workspace G0 pass. LSP diagnostics timed out twice; compiler and workspace strict Clippy are clean.

- Strict Clippy requires explicit early `drop` of parser-fault mutex guards before returning a copied decision. For a `#[non_exhaustive]` token enum, `let Token::Word(word) = token else { return None; }` avoids duplicate wildcard match arms while remaining future-compatible.

## [2026-11-06] Catalog typing keyword correction
- Poppler Arguments tables must be parsed by column provenance, not token shape alone: genuine Value-column continuations can cross description wraps and repeated page headings. Preserve the active keyword row across those lines, update its Value-column offset from repeated headings, and reject argument-name matches beyond the table's left argument column so description prose cannot overwrite typed rows.

The 5.1 KN-1811 regression guard pins required subsets for 11 high-risk command arguments rather than copying whole generated inventories.

## [2026-11-07] Intermittent serve MCP timeout
- `rmcp-server-kit` performs one Argon2id verification per configured API-key slot on every authenticated request. With two fixture keys and three MCP POSTs, unoptimized debug crypto dominates fixture and request latency.
- Dev-package optimization for `argon2` and `blake2` is the narrow cause-level fix: under four-way CPU load, fixture time fell from 15.25 s to 0.47 s, MCP requests to 0.27-0.34 s, and user CPU from 14.12 s to 1.32 s.
- Timing headers separately from body completion distinguishes KDF delay from SSE lifetime; tools/list body completion added only 36 ms after headers.
- The final stress protocol ran both affected tests in every iteration, passed 30/30, and used trap-based PID cleanup so no CPU hog remained.

RCI console GET counts as Read, DELETE as Destructive; the CLI `unvalidated` escape also needs ManageRouters (RawAuthorization only when the box is checked).

- askama: a `MessageKey` field in an inline template is `t(*field)`; `if let Some(key) = option_field` binds `&MessageKey`, so `t(**key)`. Templates included from another module's template need accessor methods (private fields resolve in the including struct's module).
- htmx: `hx-swap-oob="beforeend:#toasts"` appends the element's children; HX-Retarget/HX-Reswap route a confirmation into the shared dialog, whose form re-posts with `hx-include="#explorer-form"` so secrets are never echoed back; `sse-close="done"` stops EventSource reconnects after a job ends; `<details hx-trigger="toggle once">` lazy-loads group pages.
- Catalog facts (Catalog per router, 600 s TTL on the WebState clock) make validation router-free: a rejected request sends nothing. The incident test warms the cache with a page GET; disabling the cache made it fail on the request counter (27 vs 9), so the assertion can fail.
- The workspace source guard (`crates/kcc/tests/source_guards.rs`) rejects the substring `Command::new`, so `CliCommand::new` must be imported as `CliCommand as ValidatedCli` (kcc-domain does the same).
- `#[tokio::test(start_paused = true)]` + JobRunner: open the job stream before cancelling and read until `event: done`; by then the router's DELETE has been logged, so the stop test needs no polling.
- Visual QA: scratch `#[ignore]` harness serving `web_router` with the CSP header on 127.0.0.1:18433 (started with `setsid -f`, deleted afterwards), podman `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.64.1-noble`; evidence `.omo/evidence/task-42-keenetic-control-center-{en-light-desktop,uk-dark-phone}-{search,detail,confirm,response}.png` and `-error.png`; no CSP violations, no sideways overflow at 1280 and 390.

## [2026-11-06] Todo 44 admin pages
- A shared `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` across worktrees is unsafe: cargo hashes path packages by their workspace-relative path, so every worktree writes the same artifact names and fingerprints compare mtimes only. `cargo xtask i18n-gen` in t44 ran the xtask binary built in t25 (its `env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")` points at t25) and rewrote t25's `keys.rs` (content-identical, mtime only); an integration test of t44 linked a kcc-app lib built from older sources. Use a private target dir per worktree (t44 used `/home/andrico/kcc-wt/t44/target`), or `touch` every changed source before each build.
- Editing a source while a background build compiles that crate can leave a fingerprint newer than the edit: touch edited files before the verification build.
- askama binds macro arguments in parameter order: a bare identifier equal to an earlier parameter name (`name` passed as `value` to `ui::field(id, name, label, value, ...)`) evaluates to that parameter (the literal "name"), not the struct field. Name template fields differently from the macro parameters (`key_name`, `role_name`); the dead-code lint exposed it.
- CSS: `.page` is a grid with an implicit `auto` column, so a table's min-content (or a `min-inline-size`) inside a grid item widened the page past 390 px; admin grids use `grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr)`. A `.visually-hidden` (position: absolute) header inside `.table-wrap` escaped the scroll clip and widened the document until the wrapper got `position: relative`.
- htmx 2.0.11: `HX-Trigger-After-Swap` fires on the requesting element, or on `body` when it left the DOM. A dialog form answering `HX-Reswap: none` with out-of-band elements (`hx-swap-oob="true"` region; `hx-swap-oob="beforeend:#toasts"` wrapper whose children are appended) updates the page without touching the dialog; `kcc:dialog-close` then closes it, and the dialog's `close` handler empties its content (verified with Playwright: the one-time API key leaves the DOM).
- rmcp-server-kit `serve_with_listener(listener, config, factory, ready_tx, shutdown)` sends `ready_tx` before `run_server` calls `on_reload_ready`: wait for an own signal from the reload callback before relying on the handle. `ReloadHandle` is not `Clone`: keep it in one `Arc<OnceLock<ReloadHandle>>` borrowed by the SIGHUP task and the Web UI bridge. Bearer verification loads the key list per request, so an empty startup key list plus hot reload authenticates.
- `axum::Form` (serde_urlencoded) cannot collect repeated keys into a `Vec`: the role form has one `Option<String>` checkbox field per permission, named after its serde name.
- Verification: `cargo test -p kcc-web --test admin_pages` 10/10, mutation-checked (dominance, forced change, hot reload, tamper detection each turned their test red); full G0 + assets-check + i18n-check green. Evidence `.omo/evidence/task-44-keenetic-control-center{.log,-*.png,-playwright.log}`.

- Public protected-write APIs now present and integration-tested in `/home/andrico/kcc-wt/tdom`: `router_users::plan_password(&RouterCtx, &str, SecretString)`, `wifi::plan_wpa_psk(&RouterCtx, &ObjectName, SecretString)`, and `wan::plan_password(&RouterCtx, &ObjectName, SecretString)`, each converted through its module's `change_request` using `CliCommand::from_template`.

- Running domain functions inside the leased change set: `apply_with<W: AsyncFnOnce(&mut ChangeSet, &RouterCtx) -> Result<Applied, WorkflowError>>`, called with `async |changes, ctx| system::reboot(changes, ctx).await.map(...)`; Send leaks through at the concrete call site.

## [2026-11-06] Todo 37 network pages (worktree wt/t37)
- askama 0.16: binding modes are the trap. Field access in call args needs `t(*obj.field)`, an `if let` binding of a field needs `t(**var)`; to stay robust, have methods return localized `String`s (`line.link_text(shell.locale())`, `level_text(...)`) and `{{ text }}` them. `{% let x = expr %}` works; a nested `{% for %}` shadows `loop.index`, so save the row's index with `{% let row = loop.index %}` first. Calling `self.method(&row)` from a loop works through deref coercion (`&&T` -> `&T`), but `loop.index0` arrives as `&usize`, so pass the row itself, not an index.
- rustfmt makes route tables and `vec![Fact::new(..).editable(..)]` lists tall: budget ~2x lines per handler and split by responsibility early (page / rows / edits / settings / order / delete). Measure with `awk '!/^[[:space:]]*$/ && !/^[[:space:]]*(\/\/|#|--)/'`.
- Python/sed edits of `use crate::{...}` blocks silently miss after `cargo fmt` reflows them: re-grep after every scripted edit, or replace the whole block by regex. `pkill -f <pattern>` kills the tool's own shell when the pattern occurs in the command line: kill by `pgrep -f "^/abs/path/to/binary"` PIDs instead.
- Copying an existing target dir (`tar` of `debug/` without `incremental`) into a private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` reuses every registry dependency: only the 9 path crates recompiled (1 m 31 s instead of a cold build).

## [2026-11-06] Todo 40 system pages (worktree wt/t40)
- futures `stream::unfold` with an async closure must not capture state: `async move |s| { .. interval .. }` fails with "async closure does not implement FnMut because it captures state". Carry the captured values (locale, interval) inside the unfold state tuple and use a plain `async |state|` closure.
- askama tuple destructuring in loops (`{% for (day, label, checked) in days %}` over `Vec<(&str, MessageKey, bool)>`) binds `label` as `&&MessageKey` (`t(**label)`) but `checked` as `&bool` (`{% if *checked %}`); an `if let Some((href, label)) = method()` pattern also needs `t(**label)`.
- Deterministic job tests on paused time: the job's poll sleeps only advance when every task is idle, so a test that posts start, opens the stream and posts cancel without awaiting a timer always cancels before the second poll; the fake's DELETE is then in `request_log()` once the stream says `done`.
- The fake's `components commit` opens a 5 s reboot window on its own `SystemClock` (not tokio time); a web test can follow the commit job to its `rebooting` phase deterministically, but not to `completed` without a `MockClock` fixture.
- The fake accepts `schedule <name> ...`, `ndns ...` and `ip http proxy ...` CLI lines into the running config; `system eject`, `components check-update`, `cloud control2 ...` and `system configuration factory-reset` come back as `no such command` errors (router-refusal paths for tests).
- Killing a scratch server with `pkill -f <pattern>` also kills the tool's own shell when the pattern appears in its command line; use `pidof <binary-name>`.
- Visual QA: tables whose last cell holds a button need `vertical-align: middle` (the admin region already had it); a nine-tab bar should wrap (`.tabs--wrap`) so the current tab stays visible at 390 px.
- `kcc-testkit/tests/no_secrets.rs` scans the whole repository (locales and tests included) for RFC 1918 addresses not listed in `crates/kcc-testkit/fixtures/ALLOWED_ADDRESSES.txt`: use documentation ranges (192.0.2.0/24, 198.51.100.0/24, 203.0.113.0/24) in help texts and tests. ## Catalog synopsis typing regression fix
- The generator must complete every parsed synopsis `Argument` after reading the manual table: missing/unparseable rows use conservative name inference (`*interface*` -> Interface; count/interval/seconds/minutes/hours/days/pcp/priority/metric/group/proceed and observed `pause`/`vga-clamp` -> Integer; otherwise String). Exact legacy artifacts live in `catalog/untyped-arguments.json` and the checked-in catalog test traverses every synopsis recursively.
- Final untyped synopsis arguments are allowlist-only: 4.3 has 6 (`access-list rule::new-index`, `interface channel auto-rescan::{hh,mm}`, `nvox sip forward-if-busy::{number`, `tools ping{,6}::source-address`); 5.0 and 5.1 have those plus `show upnp pinhole::interface` (7 each).
- Master-vs-regenerated audit: 4.3 changed 59 existing types and newly typed 8 arguments; 5.0 changed 63 and newly typed 9; 5.1 changed 62 and newly typed 26. Keyword old-literal removals were 27/28/29 sets and are extracted prose/table noise; the existing 11 documented keyword-set guard remains green. Coverage is 99% for every line; largest shards are 478417/507495/517159 bytes.
- The 5.1 manual itself contains `interface mobile acq`, `interface mobile apn`, and `interface mobile force-connected`, with no corresponding `interface usb acq|apn|wwan-force-connected` headings. The three command removals are therefore a real vendor rename, not parser loss.

- `kcc serve` + browser QA: rmcp-server-kit's own Origin check rejects every browser POST (log `rejected request: Origin not allowed ... allowed=`) unless `[server] allowed_origins = ["http://127.0.0.1:<port>"]` is set in kcc.toml; the Web UI's `web_allowed_origins()` is not fed to the kit. Hermetic QA recipe that worked: AccountStore seeding from a scratch `#[ignore]` test in `crates/kcc/tests` (QA_STATE/QA_KEY env, deleted afterwards), `env -i HOME=.. XDG_CONFIG_HOME=.. XDG_STATE_HOME=.. KCC_MASTER_KEY_FILE=.. kcc serve --config ..` started with `setsid -f`, `kcc-fake-router --model KN-1811 --password-file ..`, podman playwright:v1.64.1-noble with /tmp/opencode/t42/qa/node_modules.
- kcc-migrate `Planner::plan` marks every scalar that differs between source and target as a Conflict (PlanBlocked); a raw restore therefore prepares the applier with the router's own running config as the source (empty plan) and restores the backup bytes, which `RawRestore::restore` takes separately.
- `ChangeSet::apply_cli` reads last-change after dispatch and always reports unsaved; a rollback (the router reloads/reboots) belongs in `apply_cli_terminal` (`Applied::Terminal`, "the router is carrying it out").
- The fake router reports fail-safe in `show last-change` (`fail-safe.active/remaining`), arms with `system configuration fail-safe timer reboot <s>` and treats `fail-safe rollback` as a reboot (running = startup, sessions cleared); without `show system mode` the migration preflight arms a scheduled reboot instead.
- `BackupStore::create` names a backup `<unix second>-<reason>.txt`; two creates with one reason in one second overwrite each other. The page serializes its own creates (static std Mutex) and refuses a same-second duplicate (`StoreError::Taken`); uploads get distinct reasons from the file stem (`upload-<slug>`).
- Popover menu items that post with htmx stay open unless they carry `popovertarget=<menu> popovertargetaction="hide"` too (no JS needed). A `<select>` with long option text overflows a grid column on a phone: give the column `minmax(0, 1fr)` and the select `inline-size: 100%`.
- Fluent: a duration ending in an abbreviation period (`дн.`, `Tg.`) must not end a sentence ("..180 дн.."); put `{ $value }` mid-sentence.
- clippy in this profile also flagged: `inline_trait_bounds` (use where clauses), `struct_excessive_bools` (>3 bools: keep `Controls` and add methods), `return_and_then` (`?`), `too_long_first_doc_paragraph` (also on module docs).

## 2026-11-07: Todo 24 Unit 1 routing
- Public API is intentionally module-qualified (no `pub use`): `routing::model::{Ipv4Route, Ipv6Route, RouteTableEntry}`, `routing::read::{ipv4_table, ipv6_table}`, `routing::change::{plan_add_ipv4, plan_remove_ipv4, plan_add_ipv6, plan_remove_ipv6, request, remove_ipv4}`, and `routing::descriptor::DESCRIPTOR`; `DomainId::Routing` is registered in `registry()`.
- Every plan builds a named JSON object using the catalog's exact fields and calls `Catalog::validate` before constructing `CliCommand as ValidatedCli`; no positional arrays are used. IPv4 removal includes destination, mask/host, gateway, interface, and metric when present.
- `remove_ipv4` reads a fresh pre-change table, executes through `ChangeSet::apply_cli`, scans every returned status message case-insensitively for `cleared`, and on that marker re-applies every pre-change static IPv4 route through separately catalog-validated parser requests before returning `DomainError::SafetyAbort`. A normal removal performs a fresh read and requires `before.checked_sub(after) == Some(1)`, otherwise `RouteCountMismatch`.
- Integration coverage against the real fake HTTP router passes 3/3: exact-one IPv4 removal, route-wipe abort with complete two-route restoration, and IPv6 named-field round trip. The wipe test initially restored only the requested route and failed against the second route; strengthening restoration to the complete pre-change static-route snapshot made it pass, demonstrating that the assertion catches incomplete recovery.
- Verification: dated-nightly workspace format check, `cargo clippy -p kcc-domain --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`, `cargo build -p kcc-domain --all-features`, and `cargo test -p kcc-domain --test routing` pass. LSP diagnostics timed out twice; compiler and strict Clippy diagnostics are clean. Routing files are split by responsibility and each is below 200 pure LOC (root 4, model 52, read 12, descriptor 34, change 187; test 68).

- `.visually-hidden` (absolute) inside a `.table-wrap` with no positioned ancestor escapes the sideways scroller and widens the document; mobile Chrome then zooms out (innerWidth 584 at a 390 px viewport) and `scrollWidth - innerWidth` still reads 0. Fixed for the wizard review (`position: relative` on its `.table-wrap`); other pages with visually hidden legends in tables may have it too.
- clippy `missing_const_for_fn` false positive: a builder replacing a `String` field cannot be const (E0493); restructure (struct update) instead of expecting.
- `tokio::select!` expands to `%`, which `integer_division_remainder_used` denies even with `biased;`: use `futures_util::future::select` of pinned futures.
- `significant_drop_tightening` fires for any struct holding a tokio guard; drop it after the last use or put a narrow `#[expect]` where every branch ends using it.
- Browser QA: the session keeps the locale it had at sign-in (sign in again after `/ui/account/locale`); an `HX-Redirect` to the same URL makes `waitForURL` resolve at once (wait for `load` and the content); the shared dialog has two submit buttons (its Close form first), target `.dialog__actions button`.

## 2026-11-07: BackupStore exclusive creation and automatic retention (wt/tapp)
- Public API: `BackupRetention::new(max_age: Duration, keep_last: u32)`, `BackupStore::with_retention`, and `BackupStore::with_clock`. `BackupStore::new` remains no-pruning for existing callers; `kcc serve` supplies `config.backups.max_age` and `keep_last` to the shared store.
- Creation holds the existing per-router cross-process lock and publishes through a private temp file plus `hard_link`, so an existing backup is never replaced. Collisions use `<second>-<sequence>-<reason>.txt`, with the unsuffixed name first; validation accepts both old and new names, and listing orders equal-second backups by sequence.
- Automatic retention runs after each successful create, protects the newly created name, deletes oldest-first, and applies both nonzero limits. `keep_last = 0` and `max_age = 0s` disable their respective limits. Prune failure emits a structured warning with router and display-safe error while create still returns success.
- Deterministic integration coverage uses an injected atomic clock: same-second content preservation, eight simultaneous tasks, newest-three count pruning, age pruning, disabled limits, and logged non-fatal prune failure. Required evidence: `.omo/evidence/task-backups-keenetic-control-center.log` (6/6 green).
- Mutation receipts: replacing exclusive hard-link publication with overwrite rename made same-second tests red (`1 != 1`; concurrent list 1 vs 8). ## 2026-11-07: Todo 62(a-b) audit append lock and admin password reset (wt/t62a)
- Audit appends reuse `StateStore::try_lock("audit.jsonl")`; the writer retries 200 times at 10 ms (2 s maximum), then returns typed `AuditError::AppendLockTimeout`. Under the lock it re-discovers and verifies every audit file, derives the disk tail, reopens the active append handle, rotates if needed, appends, and `sync_data`s before releasing the lock. The in-process mpsc writer remains the fast serialization path and never blocks a Tokio worker on an OS lock.
- `audit_multiprocess` re-executes its own test binary as four children, gates their start through stdin, and has each append 25 entries. The seeded entry plus all 100 child entries verify as one contiguous chain. Releasing the lock immediately after acquisition made the test red; restoring produced byte-identical SHA-256 hashes for both audit source files and a green test. Evidence is `task-62a-keenetic-control-center.log`.
- `AccountStore::reset_password((&actor_username, &Role), target, &SecretString)` requires `ManageUsers`, refuses self-reset, and permits resetting an enabled admin only from another persisted enabled admin. It uses the existing Argon2id parameters and password policy, resets failure/lockout state, and sets `must_change_password=true`; missing targets return `false`. AccountStore owns neither Web sessions nor auditing, so callers retain those responsibilities like existing admin actions.
- `kcc user passwd <user>` compares `<user>` with the CLI principal's OS account name: self changes still require `--current-password-file`/prompt and call `change_password`; another account uses `reset_password` with the established new-secret source (`--password-file`, `KCC_PASSWORD`, hidden prompt) and audits `cli.user_password_reset` with username only. Viewer refusal exits 10; password validation exits 4; replacement secrets are absent from stdout, stderr, and audit.
- Verification: focused app/CLI tests and Clippy passed; full G0 passed including workspace tests with `--no-fail-fast` and i18n-check. Rust LSP timed out on all attempts, so compiler/Clippy diagnostics are authoritative. `cli/accounts.rs` is 217 pure LOC (warning band); split it before adding another account/API-key feature. The inherited `audit.rs` and `accounts.rs` remain oversized, while new responsibilities live in small child modules.

## 2026-11-07: Todo 63 firewall domain fidelity (wt/t63)
- `firewall::change::Plan` owns the catalog-validated operation plus a firewall-specific verification contract. Rendering is one domain function used by MCP and the Web rule add/remove/property paths; rule lines include action, protocol, source/destination address and mask, exact/range ports, and property lines include disable, schedule, description and order.
- Full rule identity is action + protocol + source address/mask + source exact/range port + destination address/mask + destination exact/range port. Property verification additionally pins the pre-change rule at its index and compares the requested disabled/schedule/description/order result after a fresh `show rc access-list` read. Removal of an absent identity returns `DomainError::FirewallRuleAbsent` before any parser command.
- `valid_mask` now accepts the all-zero mask while retaining contiguous-mask checks; catalog tests keep `255.0.255.0` and `0.0.0.1` invalid. The exhaustive domain matrix covers 2 actions x 4 protocols x 3 source forms x 3 destination forms x 3 port forms x 2 disabled states x 2 schedule states (864 cases), checks the exact parser line through the redacted request log, and reads the same identity back.
- Mutation receipt: changing full-identity containment to last-address-token matching made `removal_uses_full_protocol_and_port_identity` fail with `FirewallVerificationFailed`; restoring `firewall/change/verify.rs` produced SHA-256 `f8ca276146101d2c95edfa01f92ba7aae2e7b11a3075a657852a309baab14550` and the test passed.
- The policy domain verifies `route`, `rate-limit` and `standalone`, but this row required unchanged `routing_pages` behavior; the page remains as-is and those controls stay out. Catalog `access-list rule` still has the generator warning for untyped `new-index`; domain validation plus typed rendering remains the deliberate workaround. No catalog data file was edited.
- Testkit request-log observation now records only non-secret parser lines; secret-bearing command fields remain omitted. This additive assumed observation contract is documented in `crates/kcc-testkit/BEHAVIOUR.md`.

## 2026-11-07: Todo 64 domain and job write defects (wt/t64)
- Running jobs hold a `StateStore::try_lock("job-owner-<id>")` guard for their task lifetime. Startup recovery probes that sidecar: a held lock means a live process still owns the job and leaves it Running; an available lock means the owner is gone and the record becomes Interrupted. The persisted `JobRecord` schema is unchanged, so old records remain readable and are orphaned only when no owner lock is held. A child re-exec test proves a second runner preserves the live job and recovers it after abrupt child termination.
- Password changes now take the active profile login at the domain boundary and return `ProtectedAccount` before planning when names match. CLI checks the profile before connecting, exits 10, sends zero router requests, and tells the operator to change the router password then run `kcc router edit --new-password`; MCP uses the same typed refusal. This deliberately does not automate credential-file replacement or rollback.
- Firewall binding verification now reads fresh `show rc interface <name>` data and compares exact ACL plus `in`/`out` direction and expected presence. The fake projection of `interface <name> ip access-group <acl> in|out` is additive and **assumed**; a silent parser no-op returns `FirewallVerificationFailed`.
- FQDN include/exclude validate only the catalog's `address` leaf while retaining the typed group name for CLI context rendering. DHCP pool planning omits optional DNS and bind commands instead of inventing `disable`; CLI `--dns` and `--interface` are optional. Focused evidence is `.omo/evidence/task-64-keenetic-control-center.log`; full G0 plus `i18n-check` passed. LSP timed out, so compiler and strict Clippy are the diagnostics authority.

## 2026-11-07: Todo 49 release builds (wt/t49)
- Layout: `.github/workflows/release.yml` (on `push: tags v*` + `workflow_dispatch`), jobs `meta` (version = tag without `v`, must equal `crates/kcc/Cargo.toml` version; dispatch gives `<crate>-dryrun.<sha12>`) -> `build` matrix -> `checksums` (downloads `kcc-*` artifacts, concatenates per-leg `SHA256SUMS`, asserts 4 lines, `--verify`, uploads artifact `release`) -> `release` (only `github.event_name == 'push'` on `refs/tags/v*`, the only job with `contents: write`; `gh release create --verify-tag --generate-notes`, `--prerelease` when the version has a `-`). A dispatch run therefore stops at the `release` artifact and never creates a release.
- Matrix: x86_64/aarch64-unknown-linux-musl on ubuntu-latest via `cargo zigbuild` (mlugg/setup-zig v2.2.1 zig 0.17.1, cargo-zigbuild@0.24.1 via taiki-e/install-action), aarch64-apple-darwin on macos-14 and x86_64-pc-windows-msvc on windows-latest via plain `cargo build`. All `--release --locked -p kcc`. No rust-cache in the release workflow (cache-poisoning). New pins (SHA from `gh api repos/<r>/git/ref/tags/<t>`, all lightweight tags on commits): upload-artifact v7.0.2 cf430e03..., download-artifact v8.0.2 9000827c..., setup-zig v2.2.1 d1434d08....
- Artifacts: `kcc-<version>-<target>.tar.gz` (`.zip` for windows) with top dir `kcc-<version>-<target>/` holding `kcc[.exe]`, LICENSE-APACHE, LICENSE-MIT, README.md, kcc.example.toml; plus `SHA256SUMS`. `scripts/package-release.sh <version> <target> <bin> <out>` replaces an existing line for the same archive (idempotent re-run); `--verify <dir>` runs `sha256sum -c` (falls back to `shasum -a 256` on macOS, `7z` when `zip` is absent on the Windows runner).
- Verified locally: actionlint 1.7.12 + shellcheck 0.11.0 clean; both musl builds (~17 min each on the shared 4-core host, thin LTO + codegen-units=1) are `statically linked, stripped`, x86_64 `ldd` says not a dynamic executable; `kcc --version` = `kcc 0.1.0`; packaging, `sha256sum -c` OK and the corrupted-copy failure case. CI-only/unverified until the first run: the macOS and Windows legs, the zip/7z branch, setup-zig on the runner, artifact download layout, and the `release` job.
- Dry run: Actions -> Release -> Run workflow (any branch); collect the `release` artifact. Local: `cargo zigbuild --release --locked --target x86_64-unknown-linux-musl -p kcc && scripts/package-release.sh 0.1.0 x86_64-unknown-linux-musl target/x86_64-unknown-linux-musl/release/kcc dist && scripts/package-release.sh --verify dist`.
- `kcc.example.toml` sets every kcc-section key to its default; `crates/kcc-app/tests/example_config.rs` asserts parse + `validate()` + per-section equality with `Config::default()` (mutating `keep_last` or misspelling a key turns it red). Kit sections show only listen_addr/port and log_level/format.
- Root `Cargo.toml` is excluded from taplo (pinned to the R-GUIDE template); `[profile.release]` added after the dev overrides, profile-eq unaffected (it compares lints only).

## [2026-11-08] Todo 70: signed-in language, router-aware navigation, bare sign-in shell, segments list/delete (wt/t70)
- Language: `POST /ui/account/locale` now also calls `SessionStore::set_user_locale(username, locale)` (every live session of that account, nobody else's); the signed-in masthead menu carries `data-kcc-locale-action="/ui/account/locale"` and `app.js` posts the choice with the CSRF header before reloading (the `kcc_lang` cookie is still written for the sign-in page). Signing in/out through htmx now answers `HX-Redirect` (`auth::go_to`): a body swap kept the anonymous page's `<meta name="csrf-token">`, so the first POST after an htmx sign-in was refused 403. `htmx:afterSwap` of the whole body copies `<html lang>` from the response.
- Navigation rules (`Shell::nav`, `Section::router_page`/`href_on`): Clients, Network, Wi-Fi, Routing, Firewall, DNS, VPN, System link to `/ui/r/{router}/<page>` (System -> `system/general`) for the router selected in the fleet switcher, else the only router in it (the switcher is `fleet.visible(role)`, so scoped roles count only their routers); otherwise `/ui/<section>`, which renders the router picker (`src/pages/picker.rs`, `pages/navpicker.html`; Clients keeps its own landing). Dashboard/Configuration/Advanced/Migration/Presets/Admin unchanged. `web-section-unavailable` and `pages/section.html` are gone; `/ui/dashboard` and unknown slugs answer 404 as before.
- Bare shell: `prefs::shell` (every signed-out page) returns `Shell::signed_out()`; `base.html` drops the Menu button, fleet context and side nav when `!shell.signed_in()`.
- Segments: `clients_pages/segment_list.rs` (`Region::read` via `kcc_domain::segments::read_segments`, `read_back` for the out-of-band list after create/delete), `clients_pages/segment_delete.rs` (`GET|POST /ui/r/{router}/segments/delete?id=&seen=`; `TypedNameDialog::with_list` shows `delete_summary().owned_contexts`/`preserved_ports`; `seen` = 8-byte SHA-256 fingerprint of the summary, a changed segment answers 422 with the new summary; Write permission; `delete_discovered` inside `run_with`). Tests: `tests/segments_page.rs` (create -> list -> delete restores `settings_snapshot()` and `running_config_text()`; viewer sees no Delete and gets 403; wrong/hidden name 422 audited `confirmation_mismatch`; partial segment flagged and deletable).
- GOTCHA (disk full): `cargo fmt` that hits ENOSPC leaves the file it was writing truncated at a 4096-byte boundary (here `segment_delete.rs` ended mid-statement). Recovery used the opencode session DB (`~/.local/share/opencode/opencode.db`, table `part`, tool `input` of the write/edit calls), replaying the recorded edits and checking that rustfmt of the result is byte-identical to the surviving 4096 bytes.
- Gaps/follow-ups: no independent visual-QA reviewer ran (harness has no oracle agent; verdicts in the task-70 log are self-review). Kit-wide: typed-name dialogs submit on Enter once the name matches, and the Done toast repeats what the in-place list shows. The bare-shell layout relies on `:has()`; router-picker links are text-sized (WCAG 2.5.8 spacing exception) - both recorded in DESIGN.md section "todo 70". The menu's POST failure is not reported (the reload just shows the old language).

## [2026-11-10] Todo 99 follow-up (W=/home/andrico/kcc-wt/t99)

## [2026-11-11] Todo 115 progress (W=/home/andrico/kcc-wt/t115)

- Raw whole-interface removal (`interface`, POST `no`, DELETE, and CLI `no interface`) and `system mode` are Management. A destructive spelling keeps `Risk::Destructive` while `contains_management()` stays true, so every raw surface requires `Destructive` and runs the mode-aware recovery bracket.
- Candidate decisions:
  - `interface <if> role`: Management; changing LAN/WAN role can replace the management path.
  - `interface <if> ipv6 address`: Management; kcc may be connected over that address.
  - `interface <if> ip dhcp client ...`: Management for the raw route; DHCP-client route/name-server behavior can replace the return path, and raw classification has no runtime interface topology.
  - `ip hotspot ... deny|policy`: Write; these client access/policy controls do not change kcc's router-management endpoint.
  - `interface <if> ip global`: Management; it changes default-route priority.
  - `interface <if> mac address|clone`: Management; changing link identity can break the active adjacency. Other `interface mac` radio tuning (`band`, `bssid`, `vht40`, access-list) stays Write.
  - Default `ip route`: Management; it can replace the route back to kcc. The raw route is intentionally coarser than curated routing.
  - `dns-proxy` including management-bridge assignments: Write; it changes client/router DNS policy, not the IP endpoint or return route used by kcc.
- Ordinary exclusions verified as Write: `interface description`, `interface rf`, `interface mac band`, hotspot default/interface policy, and DNS-proxy interface-profile assignment. None newly requires `Destructive`.

- Lessons: htmx 2 fires `htmx:responseError` only while `isError` stays true, so a beforeSwap rule that sets it false means the in-place refusal and the toast never both show. `HX-Trigger` runs before the swap: `kcc:dialog-close` empties `#dialog-content` before the refusal lands outside it. The fixture's first console POST runs the catalog detection (9 router requests): count requests only after a page GET warmed the facts cache. `node` here is a bun shim; `vm.runInNewContext` over a stub DOM checks app.js without a browser. Never `| tee -a EV | head`: head's exit kills tee and truncates the evidence. `module_name_repetitions` is denied (no `RefusalFragment` in `refusal`), and a public module's doc must not intra-link a `pub(crate)` child.
- Follow-up (not done here): domain handlers (firewall, segments, Wi-Fi, routers scope) could answer `errors::refusal` for `#page-outcome` to name the permission instead of the generic toast (DESIGN section 8 debt row).

## [2026-11-12] Todo 127 progress (W=/home/andrico/kcc-wt/t127)

- `Applier::rollback` now maps an unconfirmed terminal rollback request to `ApplyError::FinishFailed`, so the shared `recovery_armed()` rule reaches MCP and both Web finish surfaces. - Shared design: `Applier::rollback` still maps a terminal request that was not accepted to `FinishFailed`. Once the terminal request succeeds, a failed `wait_restored` maps router mode to `FinishFailed` (native rollback cannot be confirmed) and scheduled-reboot mode to `RollbackRestarting` (the reboot was accepted; later silence is expected). A successful restore remains `Ok(())`.

- Cause confirmed (F3 N67): the consoles gated on `risk` (Destructive for a `no` removal) but rendered `Prompt::new(Family::Commands, Risk::Management, ...)` whenever `contains_management()`, and the Management dialog has no `confirm_name` field, so the gate could never be satisfied from the browser (dialog loop, 0 router writes). The old tests posted `confirm_name=home` blindly, so they never noticed the dialog had no such field.
- Shared seam (`page_kit/stakes.rs`, new): `Stakes` = risk + whether a management command runs the request inside the recovery timer (`Stakes::new(risk)`, `Stakes::of(&ValidatedRequest)`, `with_management`); `Stakes::guard(family) -> Option<Guard>` is the ONE confirmation policy; `Guard::{Write, Management, Destructive, DestructiveManagement}` with `needs_name()` and `check(&Confirmation, router) -> Gate`. `Family::ask(stakes, &confirmation, router) -> Option<Prompt>` returns both the gate outcome (None = run) and the dialog, built from the same guard; `Prompt::new(family, guard, router)` and `with_wrong_name` are `pub(super)`, so outside `page_kit` a prompt exists only through `ask` and no site can render a dialog for a risk it did not gate on. `Family::gate(risk, ...)` stays for pages with their own dialog and goes through the same guard. `Family::dialog(guard)`: `DestructiveManagement` shares the management words.
- Combined dialog: management title + timer text (`web-explorer-confirm-management-*`, `web-kit-confirm-management-*` for domain pages), the `confirm_name` field with the help line `web-admin-confirm-help` ("Type <router> to confirm.", existing key in six locales, already reused by `typed_name.rs`), danger button. The destructive-only dialog keeps its own text (it already says "Type the router name to confirm.") and gets no help line. No new i18n keys.

## [2026-11-12] Todo 123 progress (run 3, W=/home/andrico/kcc-wt/t123)

- Reconciled the complete constructor inventory from run 2 against HEAD `be1807e`. The 47 matches remain 35 production constructors, 2 test constructors, and 10 declarations/signatures. Interface-emitting owners and verdicts are: `interfaces.rs:263` (shared verdict for down/security, ordinary up/description); `wifi/plan.rs:48,70` (shared verdict for disable/schedule, ordinary remaining leaves); `wan.rs:165,291` (DHCP/static classified, create/delete/priority and the remaining named leaves fixed Write); `vpn_clients/change.rs:59`, `vpn_clients/import.rs:111`, `vpn_clients/secrets.rs:114` (all fixed Write, with import additionally resetting aggregate risk at `import.rs:99`); `segments/step.rs:199` (one aggregate risk, currently derived only from the optional AP); `segments/ownership.rs:42` (ordinary ownership description); `firewall/change.rs:275` (binding classified by shared topology); and `kcc-web/src/dns_vpn/vpn_checks.rs:55` (rebuilds client aggregates as fixed Write and discards constituent risks). Non-interface constructors inspected in services, KeenDNS, users, routing, DHCP, NAT, logging, DNS, policies, clients, components, VPN servers, storage, mesh, system, schedules, MCP registered-operation metadata, Web DNS and user forms are not members of this invariant.
- Render identity remains detached at three important seams: `wan::change_request(name, operation)` receives a name independently of `wan::plan_*`; `vpn_clients::render::input` returns an untyped line while `change::plan` assigns unrelated fixed risk; segment delete executes the immutable discovered `SegmentView`, while CLI/MCP/Web currently gate a separate fixed Write operation. This confirms that changing individual risk literals cannot be the structural guard.
- Mechanism decision: classify the generated CLI plan, not a separately supplied request name. A domain-owned generated-plan classifier extracts every top-level `interface <target>` / `no interface <target>` identity and every `include|inherit <member>` identity from the actual rendered lines, obtains the shared `interfaces::detect_management` verdict for each distinct identity, and raises the aggregate plan risk to the maximum verdict-derived risk. VPN, WAN, Wi-Fi/interface and segment aggregate builders must enter through this classifier after rendering, so render identity and risk identity are the same bytes. This keeps ordinary tunnels/WAN/segment bridges at Write when topology says they are ordinary, unlike the deliberately coarse raw-catalog rule. The structural regression feeds generated commands to this classifier and fails when the resulting operation omits a rendered management identity; it is behavior over plans, never source text.
- Rejected alternatives: (1) changing the known hard-coded `Risk::Write` literals repeats the six-round whack-a-mole and leaves render/planning identity split; (2) deriving from `ValidatedRequest::risk` spreads the coarse raw `interface` rule to ordinary curated tunnels, explicitly forbidden; (3) a single `ClassifiedInterface` argument does not model segment plans containing bridge, VLAN, trunk port and AP identities, and still permits a renderer to use a different string; (4) catalog-only parity cannot know runtime bridge membership; (5) source-grep inventory tests are prohibited and would pin spelling rather than behavior.
- Implementation: `interfaces::management::classify_generated` extracts top-level interface identities and include/inherit members from generated lines, deduplicates them, and raises to Management when any shared verdict does. VPN planning now classifies its stored generated line; WAN create/delete/priority render first and classify those exact commands; segment create/delete classify every generated step. `SegmentView::deletion_plan` re-plans from private immutable discovered ownership, and CLI/MCP/Web use that operation risk before executing delete. VPN/WAN planners are async because topology classification performs read-only router calls; all call sites and coverage owners were updated.
- Red-first: after repairing the inherited draft's `Zeroizing<String>` display errors with `.as_str()`, `vpn_delete_and_disable_preserve_management_risk` failed on `no interface Bridge0` planned Write and `wan_lifecycle_and_priority_preserve_management_risk` failed on `interface Bridge0` planned Write. Final generated-plan parity is 3/3 green and includes segment create/delete for management and ordinary APs; ordinary Wireguard7/PPPoE7 remain Write.

Structural conclusion: only the three Web VPN seams rebuild or execute a planner result without its risk. CLI and MCP already force the complete `ChangeOp` through their shared helpers. The Web fix must likewise make VPN helpers consume complete `ChangeOp`s (or a request paired with risk produced only from those operations), so `.requests` alone cannot silently erase classification.

- Red-first retained tests: `wireguard_import_preserves_generated_interface_risk` reached behavior and failed with exit 101 because the import still reports Write for a management interface (`/home/andrico/t130-red-domain.log`). `management_tunnel_changes_are_refused_or_bracketed` plus `wireguard_import_refuses_every_existing_interface_name_without_writes` were launched together in `/home/andrico/t130-red-surfaces.log`; compilation was still running when the executor's mandatory two-status-check limit required stopping. No production fix or mutation was started.

- Final implementation: WireGuard import now assembles one complete `ChangeOp`, renders every protected/non-secret command through `CliCommand::expose_for_send`, and classifies the complete generated plan once. The private unclassified planner is no longer callable outside `vpn_clients/change.rs`; aggregate construction goes through `change::append` and final classification through `change::classify`.
- Web helper design: `vpn_checks::PlannedRequest` owns the protected request and maximum constituent risk. Aggregation merges complete `ChangeOp`s, `flow::apply` requires an explicit risk, and actions/forms/import dynamically authorize the resulting risk before mutation. Management-tunnel state/delete therefore return HTTP 403 with zero writes to operator/writer, while admin runs inside native fail-safe or scheduled-reboot brackets; ordinary tunnels remain Write.
- CLI import collision: `wireguard-import` reads the complete interface inventory and refuses any existing interface id, not only an existing VPN client. Inventory read errors propagate before planning or mutation. The existing localized `cli-name-taken` key already states the correct contract in all six locales, so no i18n files or generated keys changed.
- Green regressions: `wireguard_import_preserves_generated_interface_risk`; `vpn::management_tunnel_changes_are_refused_or_bracketed`; `secrets::wireguard_import_refuses_every_existing_interface_name_without_writes`. ## [2026-11-12] Todo 132 progress (W=/home/andrico/kcc-wt/t132)
- The classifier now runs in two passes: it first classifies every non-membership flattened leaf, derives mapped plan-created interfaces only from logical interface contexts whose own leaf disposition is `Copy` or `Remap`, then classifies `include`/`inherit` lines against that set and the target inventory. This reconciles AST contexts with `flatten`'s leaf-only output while preserving source order and fail-closed unreadable inventory behavior.
- Heuristic decision: physical names are `GigabitEthernet<n>/<n>` without a logical `/Vlan<n>` suffix, `WifiMaster<n>`, `WifiMaster<n>/AccessPoint<n>`, `FastEthernet*`, `Usb*`, `Ethernet*`, and `ISP`; every other interface context counts as logical. Sources checked were `crates/kcc-testkit/fixtures/*/show.json` for the real `GigabitEthernet0/n`, `WifiMaster0/AccessPoint0`, and `ISP` inventory names, plus `crates/kcc-migrate/rules/rules.toml` hardware-interface rules. This is deliberately documented as a name heuristic, not hardware-verified classification.
- Red-first: `logical_members_with_realistic_children_are_copied_after_creation`, realistic mapped VLAN coverage, and `absent_physical_port_contexts_do_not_create_bridge_members` failed on the old classifier for the intended `SkipHardware`/false-copy reasons.
